But I definitely get disorganized at work, like freezing up at the server station during a rush while just holding a tray. Sometimes I'll just stand there, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being plotted against and your loss of motivation been present consistently for at least six months, or do they come and go in shorter, distinct episodes?\n\nPatient: The loss of motivation and pulling away from my usual routines has been going on consistently for a little over a year now. However, the specific feelings of people plotting against me have been happening regularly for about four months. They don't really come and go in short episodes; they've been pretty constant since they started.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your daily life, such as a decline in your personal hygiene, a tendency to speak in a way that others find difficult to follow, or a lack of emotional expression when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed those kinds of issues at all. My hygiene is fine, and I don't think I have any trouble speaking clearly or showing my emotions when I talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that others around you cannot hear, or do you have any other unusual sensory experiences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do. Pretty often, especially at work and late at night, I hear my name being said or voices whispering that I'm screwing things up and don't deserve my job. This happens even when I can see there is nobody near me and the kitchen is completely quiet.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as believing that random events or ordinary things in your environment are specifically meant to send you a personal message?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like random events or ordinary things in my environment are trying to send me any kind of personal message.\n\nDoctor: When you interact with others, do you find it difficult to express your emotions or do you feel like your facial expressions and tone of voice have become more limited or 'flat' over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed if my facial expressions or voice have changed. I just know that I've been keeping to myself a lot more lately and avoiding talking to people.\n\nDoctor: When you are with your friends or family, do they ever mention that you seem less expressive, or that your face or voice seems less lively than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure because I've been avoiding my friends lately. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?"

I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the plants in the yard, and things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks don't bring me any joy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your speech, such as having trouble staying on topic, or have you experienced any periods where you felt disorganized or as if your thoughts were not your own?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any changes in my speech, and my thoughts still feel like my own. But I definitely get disorganized at work, like freezing up at the server station during a rush while just holding a tray. Sometimes I'll just stand there, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being plotted against and your loss of motivation been present consistently for at least six months, or do they come and go in shorter, distinct episodes?\n\nPatient: The loss of motivation and pulling away from my usual routines has been going on consistently for a little over a year now. However, the specific feelings of people plotting against me have been happening regularly for about four months. They don't really come and go in short episodes; they've been pretty constant since they started.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your daily life, such as a decline in your personal hygiene, a tendency to speak in a way that others find difficult to follow, or a lack of emotional expression when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed those kinds of issues at all. My hygiene is fine, and I don't think I have any trouble speaking clearly or showing my emotions when I talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that others around you cannot hear, or do you have any other unusual sensory experiences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do. Pretty often, especially at work and late at night, I hear my name being said or voices whispering that I'm screwing things up and don't deserve my job. This happens even when I can see there is nobody near me and the kitchen is completely quiet.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as believing that random events or ordinary things in your environment are specifically meant to send you a personal message?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like random events or ordinary things in my environment are trying to send me any kind of personal message.\n\nDoctor: When you interact with others, do you find it difficult to express your emotions or do you feel like your facial expressions and tone of voice have become more limited or 'flat' over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed if my facial expressions or voice have changed. I just know that I've been keeping to myself a lot more lately and avoiding talking to people.\n\nDoctor: When you are with your friends or family, do they ever mention that you seem less expressive, or that your face or voice seems less lively than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure because I've been avoiding my friends lately. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my husband hasn't specifically mentioned my face or voice changing. He has definitely noticed that I've been very quiet, tired, and withdrawn lately, but he hasn't said anything about my expressions.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found it increasingly difficult to initiate tasks, complete chores, or feel motivated to do things you previously enjoyed, even when you aren't feeling particularly sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, that has been a major struggle for about a year now, and I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the backyard plants because everything feels like too much effort. I've lost interest in things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks. I've even had to let my husband handle all the chores, bills, errands, and social interactions because I just can't keep up anymore."

It's gotten to the point where I just can't shake the feeling that they are trying to get me fired.\n\nDoctor: Besides these thoughts about your coworkers, have you experienced any significant periods where your mood felt extremely low or unusually high and energetic?\n\nPatient: I've never had times where I felt unusually high or energetic. But I have been feeling really flat, low on energy, and lacking motivation lately. I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the plants in the yard, and things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks don't bring me any joy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your speech, such as having trouble staying on topic, or have you experienced any periods where you felt disorganized or as if your thoughts were not your own?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any changes in my speech, and my thoughts still feel like my own. However, the specific feelings of people plotting against me have been happening regularly for about four months. They don't really come and go in short episodes; they've been pretty constant since they started.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your daily life, such as a decline in your personal hygiene, a tendency to speak in a way that others find difficult to follow, or a lack of emotional expression when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed those kinds of issues at all. My hygiene is fine, and I don't think I have any trouble speaking clearly or showing my emotions when I talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that others around you cannot hear, or do you have any other unusual sensory experiences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do. Pretty often, especially at work and late at night, I hear my name being said or voices whispering that I'm screwing things up and don't deserve my job. This happens even when I can see there is nobody near me and the kitchen is completely quiet.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as believing that random events or ordinary things in your environment are specifically meant to send you a personal message?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like random events or ordinary things in my environment are trying to send me any kind of personal message.\n\nDoctor: When you interact with others, do you find it difficult to express your emotions or do you feel like your facial expressions and tone of voice have become more limited or 'flat' over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed if my facial expressions or voice have changed. I just know that I've been keeping to myself a lot more lately and avoiding talking to people.\n\nDoctor: When you are with your friends or family, do they ever mention that you seem less expressive, or that your face or voice seems less lively than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure because I've been avoiding my friends lately. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my husband hasn't specifically mentioned my face or voice changing. He has definitely noticed that I've been very quiet, tired, and withdrawn lately, but he hasn't said anything about my expressions.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found it increasingly difficult to initiate tasks, complete chores, or feel motivated to do things you previously enjoyed, even when you aren't feeling particularly sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, that has been a major struggle for about a year now, and I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the backyard plants because everything feels like too much effort. I've lost interest in things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks. I've even had to let my husband handle all the chores, bills, errands, and social interactions because I just can't keep up anymore.\n\nDoctor: Since these symptoms began, have you ever experienced a period where your belief that people are plotting against you was the only major symptom, without any of the low energy or lack of motivation you described?\n\nPatient: No, the low energy and lack of motivation actually started first, about a year ago, while the paranoid feelings only began around four months ago. I've never experienced the plotting beliefs on their own, because that low energy and withdrawal have been constant the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your speech ever becomes disorganized, rambling, or difficult for others to follow, or if you have noticed any odd or bizarre behaviors that others have pointed out to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed my speech being disorganized or rambling, but I definitely do some odd things at work that others have noticed. During busy shifts, I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station just staring at tickets, or I'll start pacing back and forth and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing my tasks.\n\nDoctor: During the entire year that you have been struggling with low energy and lack of motivation, have you ever experienced a period of at least two weeks where your mood was so low that you felt hopeless or considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt hopeless or had thoughts of harming myself. It's gotten to the point where I just can't shake the feeling that they are trying to get me fired.\n\nDoctor: Besides these thoughts about your coworkers, have you experienced any significant periods where your mood felt extremely low or unusually high and energetic?\n\nPatient: I've never had times where I felt unusually high or energetic. But I have been feeling really flat, low on energy, and lacking motivation lately. I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the plants in the yard, and things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks don't bring me any joy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your speech, such as having trouble staying on topic, or have you experienced any periods where you felt disorganized or as if your thoughts were not your own?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any changes in my speech, and my thoughts still feel like my own. But I definitely get disorganized at work, like freezing up at the server station during a rush while just holding a tray. Sometimes I'll just stand there, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being plotted against and your loss of motivation been present consistently for at least six months, or do they come and go in shorter, distinct episodes?\n\nPatient: The loss of motivation and pulling away from my usual routines has been going on consistently for a little over a year now. However, the specific feelings of people plotting against me have been happening regularly for about four months. They don't really come and go in short episodes; they've been pretty constant since they started.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your daily life, such as a decline in your personal hygiene, a tendency to speak in a way that others find difficult to follow, or a lack of emotional expression when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed those kinds of issues at all. My hygiene is fine, and I don't think I have any trouble speaking clearly or showing my emotions when I talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that others around you cannot hear, or do you have any other unusual sensory experiences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do. Pretty often, especially at work and late at night, I hear my name being said or voices whispering that I'm screwing things up and don't deserve my job. This happens even when I can see there is nobody near me and the kitchen is completely quiet.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as believing that random events or ordinary things in your environment are specifically meant to send you a personal message?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like random events or ordinary things in my environment are trying to send me any kind of personal message.\n\nDoctor: When you interact with others, do you find it difficult to express your emotions or do you feel like your facial expressions and tone of voice have become more limited or 'flat' over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed if my facial expressions or voice have changed. I just know that I've been keeping to myself a lot more lately and avoiding talking to people.\n\nDoctor: When you are with your friends or family, do they ever mention that you seem less expressive, or that your face or voice seems less lively than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure because I've been avoiding my friends lately. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my husband hasn't specifically mentioned my face or voice changing. He has definitely noticed that I've been very quiet, tired, and withdrawn lately, but he hasn't said anything about my expressions.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found it increasingly difficult to initiate tasks, complete chores, or feel motivated to do things you previously enjoyed, even when you aren't feeling particularly sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, that has been a major struggle for about a year now, and I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the backyard plants because everything feels like too much effort. I've lost interest in things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks. I've even had to let my husband handle all the chores, bills, errands, and social interactions because I just can't keep up anymore.\n\nDoctor: Since these symptoms began, have you ever experienced a period where your belief that people are plotting against you was the only major symptom, without any of the low energy or lack of motivation you described?\n\nPatient: No, the low energy and lack of motivation actually started first, about a year ago, while the paranoid feelings only began around four months ago. I've never experienced the plotting beliefs on their own, because that low energy and withdrawal have been constant the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your speech ever becomes disorganized, rambling, or difficult for others to follow, or if you have noticed any odd or bizarre behaviors that others have pointed out to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed my speech being disorganized or rambling, but I definitely do some odd things at work that others have noticed. During busy shifts, I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station just staring at tickets, or I'll start pacing back and forth and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing my tasks.\n\nDoctor: During the entire year that you have been struggling with low energy and lack of motivation, have you ever experienced a period of at least two weeks where your mood was so low that you felt hopeless or considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt hopeless or had thoughts of harming myself. It's not really a deep sadness like that, but more of a complete lack of energy, motivation, and interest in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have a lack of emotional expression, such as your face appearing 'blank' or your voice sounding monotone, even when you are trying to communicate?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and nobody has specifically mentioned that to me. I haven't noticed my face or voice being blank or monotone myself, so I don't think it's been an issue."

But I have been feeling really flat, low on energy, and lacking motivation lately. I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the plants in the yard, and things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks don't bring me any joy anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your speech, such as having trouble staying on topic, or have you experienced any periods where you felt disorganized or as if your thoughts were not your own?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any changes in my speech, and my thoughts still feel like my own. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my husband hasn't specifically mentioned my face or voice changing. He has definitely noticed that I've been very quiet, tired, and withdrawn lately, but he hasn't said anything about my expressions.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found it increasingly difficult to initiate tasks, complete chores, or feel motivated to do things you previously enjoyed, even when you aren't feeling particularly sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, that has been a major struggle for about a year now, and I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the backyard plants because everything feels like too much effort. I've lost interest in things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks. I've even had to let my husband handle all the chores, bills, errands, and social interactions because I just can't keep up anymore.\n\nDoctor: Since these symptoms began, have you ever experienced a period where your belief that people are plotting against you was the only major symptom, without any of the low energy or lack of motivation you described?\n\nPatient: No, the low energy and lack of motivation actually started first, about a year ago, while the paranoid feelings only began around four months ago. I've never experienced the plotting beliefs on their own, because that low energy and withdrawal have been constant the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your speech ever becomes disorganized, rambling, or difficult for others to follow, or if you have noticed any odd or bizarre behaviors that others have pointed out to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed my speech being disorganized or rambling, but I definitely do some odd things at work that others have noticed. During busy shifts, I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station just staring at tickets, or I'll start pacing back and forth and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing my tasks.\n\nDoctor: During the entire year that you have been struggling with low energy and lack of motivation, have you ever experienced a period of at least two weeks where your mood was so low that you felt hopeless or considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt hopeless or had thoughts of harming myself. It's not really a deep sadness like that, but more of a complete lack of energy, motivation, and interest in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have a lack of emotional expression, such as your face appearing 'blank' or your voice sounding monotone, even when you are trying to communicate?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and nobody has specifically mentioned that to me. I haven't noticed my face or voice being blank or monotone myself, so I don't think it's been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever mentioned that your movements seem unusual, such as appearing stiff, repetitive, or suddenly freezing in place for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: Yes, my manager and coworkers have definitely noticed and brought it up during busy shifts. I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station holding a tray while people are trying to talk to me, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware when I get overwhelmed. It's like I just can't move or do what I'm supposed to do in those moments."

During busy shifts, I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station just staring at tickets, or I'll start pacing back and forth and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing my tasks.\n\nDoctor: During the entire year that you have been struggling with low energy and lack of motivation, have you ever experienced a period of at least two weeks where your mood was so low that you felt hopeless or considered harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt hopeless or had thoughts of harming myself. It's not really a deep sadness like that, but more of a complete lack of energy, motivation, and interest in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have a lack of emotional expression, such as your face appearing 'blank' or your voice sounding monotone, even when you are trying to communicate?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and nobody has specifically mentioned that to me. I haven't noticed my face or voice being blank or monotone myself, so I don't think it's been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever mentioned that your movements seem unusual, such as appearing stiff, repetitive, or suddenly freezing in place for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: Yes, my manager and coworkers have definitely noticed and brought it up during busy shifts. I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station holding a tray while people are trying to talk to me, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware when I get overwhelmed. It's like I just can't move or do what I'm supposed to do in those moments.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze or perform those repetitive motions, do you feel like you are consciously choosing to do them, or does it feel like your body is stuck or moving on its own without your control?\n\nPatient: It definitely feels completely out of my control, like I'm totally stuck and unable to move when I freeze, even though I know I have orders to run. When I pace or rearrange the silverware, it feels like I'm doing it automatically, almost as if my hands are just moving on their own while my mind is completely blank."

But I definitely get disorganized at work, like freezing up at the server station during a rush while just holding a tray. Sometimes I'll just stand there, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware instead of doing what I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings of being plotted against and your loss of motivation been present consistently for at least six months, or do they come and go in shorter, distinct episodes?\n\nPatient: The loss of motivation and pulling away from my usual routines has been going on consistently for a little over a year now. However, the specific feelings of people plotting against me have been happening regularly for about four months. They don't really come and go in short episodes; they've been pretty constant since they started.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your daily life, such as a decline in your personal hygiene, a tendency to speak in a way that others find difficult to follow, or a lack of emotional expression when talking to others?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed those kinds of issues at all. My hygiene is fine, and I don't think I have any trouble speaking clearly or showing my emotions when I talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices or sounds that others around you cannot hear, or do you have any other unusual sensory experiences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I actually do. Pretty often, especially at work and late at night, I hear my name being said or voices whispering that I'm screwing things up and don't deserve my job. This happens even when I can see there is nobody near me and the kitchen is completely quiet.\n\nDoctor: Besides the voices and the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed any other unusual experiences, such as believing that random events or ordinary things in your environment are specifically meant to send you a personal message?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never felt like random events or ordinary things in my environment are trying to send me any kind of personal message.\n\nDoctor: When you interact with others, do you find it difficult to express your emotions or do you feel like your facial expressions and tone of voice have become more limited or 'flat' over the past year?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and I haven't noticed if my facial expressions or voice have changed. I just know that I've been keeping to myself a lot more lately and avoiding talking to people.\n\nDoctor: When you are with your friends or family, do they ever mention that you seem less expressive, or that your face or voice seems less lively than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure because I've been avoiding my friends lately. I also let my husband handle talking to my family, so I haven't really interacted with them enough for them to comment on how I look or sound.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have difficulty showing your feelings, or that your face and voice seem less expressive than they used to be?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, as my husband hasn't specifically mentioned my face or voice changing. He has definitely noticed that I've been very quiet, tired, and withdrawn lately, but he hasn't said anything about my expressions.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found it increasingly difficult to initiate tasks, complete chores, or feel motivated to do things you previously enjoyed, even when you aren't feeling particularly sad?\n\nPatient: Yes, that has been a major struggle for about a year now, and I mostly just lie on the couch or stare at the backyard plants because everything feels like too much effort. I've lost interest in things I used to love like reading and sunrise beach walks. I've even had to let my husband handle all the chores, bills, errands, and social interactions because I just can't keep up anymore.\n\nDoctor: Since these symptoms began, have you ever experienced a period where your belief that people are plotting against you was the only major symptom, without any of the low energy or lack of motivation you described?\n\nPatient: No, the low energy and lack of motivation actually started first, about a year ago, while the paranoid feelings only began around four months ago. I've never experienced the plotting beliefs on their own, because that low energy and withdrawal have been constant the entire time.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if your speech ever becomes disorganized, rambling, or difficult for others to follow, or if you have noticed any odd or bizarre behaviors that others have pointed out to you?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed my speech being disorganized or rambling, but I definitely do some odd things at work that others have noticed. It's not really a deep sadness like that, but more of a complete lack of energy, motivation, and interest in doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you ever mentioned that you seem to have a lack of emotional expression, such as your face appearing 'blank' or your voice sounding monotone, even when you are trying to communicate?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, and nobody has specifically mentioned that to me. I haven't noticed my face or voice being blank or monotone myself, so I don't think it's been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever mentioned that your movements seem unusual, such as appearing stiff, repetitive, or suddenly freezing in place for no apparent reason?\n\nPatient: Yes, my manager and coworkers have definitely noticed and brought it up during busy shifts. I'll suddenly freeze up at the server station holding a tray while people are trying to talk to me, or I'll start pacing and repeatedly rearranging the silverware when I get overwhelmed. It's like I just can't move or do what I'm supposed to do in those moments.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze or perform those repetitive motions, do you feel like you are consciously choosing to do them, or does it feel like your body is stuck or moving on its own without your control?\n\nPatient: It definitely feels completely out of my control, like I'm totally stuck and unable to move when I freeze, even though I know I have orders to run. When I pace or rearrange the silverware, it feels like I'm doing it automatically, almost as if my hands are just moving on their own while my mind is completely blank.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze or perform these repetitive movements, are you able to respond to or communicate with the people around you, or are you completely unresponsive to them during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I'm completely unresponsive during those times, and even though people are talking directly to me, I feel totally stuck and unable to move or speak to them. I either just stand there frozen, or I get completely caught up in pacing and rearranging the silverware instead of replying. I just can't seem to break out of it to answer."

Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep. Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.  Subtypes: Unrestrained Buying Sprees, Foolish Business Investments, Sexual Indiscretions, Impulsive Risky Behavior\n[S042] Low_Self_Esteem: A persistent tendency to evaluate oneself as incompetent or worthless; chronically low self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Self-Criticism, Reduced Self-Efficacy, Chronic Sense of Inferiority, Lack of Self-Assurance\n[S043] Restrictive_Intake: Persistent restriction of energy intake relative to requirements, leading to significantly low body weight.  Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.
